Document Control
Volume 9 is the controlled visual and interaction design baseline for The Eye. It governs how the frozen product contract is 
perceived, understood, operated, challenged, decided, and verified across every role, workspace, device, locale, 
deployment profile, and consequence class.
FIELD CONTROLLED VALUE
Document The Eye - Volume 9: UI/UX Design System
Edition 1.0 / Controlled baseline
Status Approved documentation program baseline
Release date 2 August 2026
Governing authority Volume 0 - Product Constitution; Volumes 3-8; Executive Product Authority; Design System Authority
Scope Visual language, information architecture, components, patterns, responsive behavior, content, accessibility, human 
authority, explainability, degraded modes, parity, and acceptance
Requirement scheme UX-CC-NNN; immutable within this controlled edition
Conformance unit Exact requirement, component or pattern version, journey, persona and role, tenant or domain, deployment, entitlement, 
theme, locale, device, configuration, data and model versions, consequence class, period, and accepting authorities
Publication preset compact_reference_guide with controlled series override: Arial typography, editorial cover, compact tables, explicit 
diagrams, restrained navy and teal palette
Downstream inheritance Product and domain design specifications, coded component libraries, application implementations, release plans, 
acceptance plans, operational procedures, customer configuration, and Volume 10
Normative language
SHALL, MUST, SHALL NOT, and MUST NOT are binding. MAY permits an implementation choice inside the frozen design 
contract. SHOULD records a preferred expression that can be varied only when the replacement demonstrably preserves 
outcome, state, evidence, human authority, accessibility, semantic parity, and acceptance evidence.
VOLUME 9 AUTHORITY  This volume controls visual and interaction expression without changing the product contract. A local screen, component, theme, 
workflow, or model response cannot amend truth, authority, canonical ownership, deployment semantics, or constitutional invariants.

UI/UX Design System Charter
The Eye is not a chatbot, dashboard, BI platform, copilot, or reporting product. Its interfaces are governed access modes to 
a Strategic Intelligence Operating System. Volume 9 therefore controls an experience system: persistent context, canonical 
objects, truth and lifecycle state, evidence and provenance, explainable analysis, bounded agent work, explicit human 
authority, recoverable commands, and permanent organizational memory.
CHARTER ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Design outcome Every authorized user can observe, understand, predict, simulate, decide, and learn through one coherent governed 
experience.
Human sovereignty AI recommends, forecasts, simulates, reasons, and explains. Named humans make final strategic and high-impact 
decisions.
Experience integrity Context, uncertainty, provenance, decision state, consequence, correction, and degraded behavior appear at the point of 
use.
Common truth Executives, analysts, operators, experts, administrators, and governance teams receive role-appropriate depth without 
separate truths.
Design-system unit Semantic token, primitive, component, pattern, template, content rule, implementation package, evidence baseline, and 
versioned release.
Conformance A design is conformant only when every applicable UX requirement closes with evidence for the exact implementation 
scope.
Definition of design complete
• The required context, object identity, truth state, evidence, uncertainty, authority, commands, degraded behavior, and recovery are 
expressed through approved components and patterns.
• Material journeys pass visual, interaction, content, accessibility, localization, responsive, performance, security, privacy, offline, and 
deployment-parity evidence.
• Consequential actions resolve eligible authority, exact digest, consequence preview, rationale, deliberate human action, immutable 
receipt, and partial-effect recovery.
• Design tokens, themes, components, patterns, code implementations, documentation, tests, findings, exceptions, releases, and 
customer acceptance remain bidirectionally traceable.
• No unresolved conflict with the 52 constitutional invariants or Volume 8 product requirements is concealed by implementation or 
presentation.
RELEASE PRINCIPLE  Visual polish cannot compensate for a missing state, weak explanation, inaccessible journey, ambiguous command, or absent human 
authority. Those conditions are release-blocking product failures.

Executive Experience Statement
The Eye is operated through evidence-rich, role-appropriate workspaces that maintain one connected representation of the 
world and the enterprise. The experience does not ask leaders to trust a summary layer. It lets them move from material 
change to underlying evidence; from evidence to entities, relationships, memory, forecasts, scenarios, and simulations; from 
those products to explicit alternatives and dissent; and from deliberation to a non-repudiable human decision, commitment, 
outcome, and replay.
The primary organizing principle is strategic work, not screen type. A dashboard may summarize a command view, chat may 
accept bounded intent, a report may distribute an approved edition, and an API may expose governed contracts. None 
becomes the product definition, canonical owner, truth source, or authority surface. Every access mode returns to the same 
objects, versions, evidence, policy decisions, human gates, and correction history.
The visual system is calm, precise, and dense enough for serious institutional use. Navy and neutral surfaces establish 
hierarchy. Teal marks interaction and connective meaning. Semantic colors are reserved for explicit truth, severity, 
uncertainty, lifecycle, and health roles. Typography, layout, icons, charts, maps, graphs, motion, and content work as one 
language. Ornament never carries critical meaning. Color never acts alone. Confidence never resembles authority. Synthetic 
state never resembles observation.
MODE PRIMARY EXPERIENCE EXECUTIVE QUESTION
Observe Coverage, source health, evidence, blind spots, intake, and live events What is known, missing, late, or constrained?
Understand Objects, entities, relationships, Knowledge Graph, memory, claims, and 
time What does the evidence mean and how is it connected?
Predict Weak signals, warnings, risk, opportunity, forecasts, and six horizons What could happen, with what uncertainty and calibration?
Simulate Scenarios, Digital Twins, governed runs, sensitivity, and validity How might futures respond to interventions and 
assumptions?
Decide Objectives, alternatives, evidence, dissent, recommendations, human 
digest, and commitment
What will an eligible human decide, why, and with what 
consequences?
Learn Outcomes, Decision Replay, attribution, lessons, evaluated change, and 
governance
What changed, what was learned, and what proposal 
should enter control?
HUMAN DECISION SOVEREIGNTY  Every decision-active experience names the eligible human authority, preserves the exact digest reviewed, separates 
recommendation from decision, previews institutional effect, records rationale and conditions, and returns an immutable receipt.

Experience Control Model
The experience control model binds the six operating verbs to four persistent rails: operating context, truth state, trust and 
provenance, and human authority. Workspaces and components may vary by role and task, but these rails remain stable 
wherever their state can change meaning or consequence.
Figure 1. The Eye Experience Control Model. The six strategic stages remain continuous while context, truth, trust, and authority persist across every 
transition.
PERSISTENT RAIL REQUIRED INFORMATION
Context rail Tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, deployment, and effective time.
Truth rail Observed, asserted, inferred, assessed, synthetic, recommended, decided, corrected, withdrawn, and indeterminate state.
Trust rail Evidence, citation, provenance, rights, custody, freshness, quality, policy, evaluation, model and data versions, and limitations.
Authority rail Eligibility, separation, delegation, recusal, consequence, exact digest, deliberate human action, signature, receipt, override, and 
recovery.
EXPERIENCE INVARIANT  A surface is non-conformant if its visual compression, responsive adaptation, summarization, branding, automation, or failure state 
hides a material rail at the point where the user must understand or act.

1  Design Authority, Scope, and Conformance
Design Authority, Scope, and Conformance establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can make 
the controlling design baseline, accountable owners, approved exceptions, and conformance status resolvable before a 
design or release decision. It organizes authority header, baseline registry, conformance scope, acceptance evidence 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 2. Design Authority, Scope, and Conformance. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, 
human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Make the controlling design baseline, accountable owners, approved exceptions, and conformance status resolvable before a design 
or release decision.
Primary roles Executive Product Authority; Design System Authority; product, engineering, assurance, and customer acceptance leaders.
Controlled objects design baseline, design requirement, component, pattern, exception, evidence package, acceptance record.
Canonical anatomy authority header; baseline registry; conformance scope; acceptance evidence.
Primary command Register, compare, attest, supersede, or reject a design baseline without changing constitutional meaning.
Human authority Only the recorded Design System Authority may approve design-system conformance; product meaning remains governed by 
Executive Product Authority.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-01-001
The Eye SHALL present Design Authority, Scope, and Conformance as a governed 
experience that enables authorized users to make the controlling design baseline, 
accountable owners, approved exceptions, and conformance status resolvable before a 
design or release decision; authority header, baseline registry, conformance scope, 
acceptance evidence SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.
Design review + journey 
test
Approved anatomy, role walkthroughs, 
content states, visual baselines, and 
accountable design review.
UX-01-002
The experience SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile design baseline, design 
requirement, component, pattern, exception, evidence package, acceptance record; 
active tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, 
classification, effective time, object identity, freshness, provenance, and policy SHALL 
remain explicit wherever they change meaning or action.
State + trace test
Positive and negative state fixtures, 
immutable references, trace queries, policy 
decisions, and stale-state evidence.
UX-01-003
The primary command SHALL allow eligible users to register, compare, attest, 
supersede, or reject a design baseline without changing constitutional meaning; 
navigation, search, inspection, drafting, acknowledgment, review, approval, publication, 
and institutional effect SHALL remain visually and behaviorally distinct, attributable, 
recoverable, and replayable.
Interaction + command 
test
Keyboard and pointer journeys, command 
receipts, cancellation, retry, recovery, audit 
causation, and replay evidence.
UX-01-004
The experience SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: Only the recorded 
Design System Authority may approve design-system conformance; product meaning 
remains governed by Executive Product Authority. AI and automation may recommend, 
forecast, simulate, reason, explain, or execute explicitly pre-authorized reversible 
workflow steps, but SHALL NOT infer, preselect, obscure, or synthesize final human 
decision authority.
Authority + 
consequence test
Eligibility, separation, delegation, recusal, 
exact digest, consequence preview, 
deliberate action, signature, and denial 
fixtures.

ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-01-005
When a component, pattern, owner, exception, or release cannot resolve to an 
approved authority, the experience SHALL hold admission or release, preserve disputed 
work, expose affected scope, and route the conflict through the recorded authority chain; 
it SHALL NOT represent unavailable, stale, unapproved, unsupported, inaccessible, 
policy-filtered, synthetic, or incomplete state as current and complete.
Fault + degraded 
journey
Fault traces, visible degraded state, 
preserved work, disabled unsafe actions, 
accessible recovery, reconciliation, and 
closure.
UX-01-006
Acceptance SHALL demonstrate orphan-free trace queries, authority conflict fixtures, 
exception expiry, and independent acceptance for the exact design-system version, 
component and pattern versions, capability, canonical journey, persona and role, tenant 
or domain, deployment profile, entitlement, theme, locale, device, assistive technology, 
configuration, data and model versions, consequence class, test period, and named 
accepting authorities.
Independent 
conformance review
Trace-complete evidence package, visual 
and interaction baselines, accessibility 
results, fault evidence, findings closure, and 
signed acceptance.
Failure and degraded experience
PRODUCT CONDITION REQUIRED EXPERIENCE BEHAVIOR
A component, pattern, owner, exception, or release 
cannot resolve to an approved authority.
Hold admission or release, preserve disputed work, expose affected scope, and route the conflict through the 
recorded authority chain.
Identity, role, authority, policy, classification, purpose, 
or exact command scope cannot be established.
Preserve work and context, deny the consequential effect, identify the unresolved condition, and route eligible 
accountable resolution.
Data, model, agent, integration, projection, network, 
or service state is partial, stale, unavailable, 
inaccessible, or recovering.
Declare visible degraded state, distinguish available from unavailable behavior, retain evidence and checkpoints, 
and recover through verified reconciliation.
Acceptance evidence
• End-to-end evidence demonstrates orphan-free trace queries, authority conflict fixtures, exception expiry, and independent acceptance.
• Positive, negative, stale-state, unauthorized-role, cross-tenant, correction, replay, and recovery fixtures preserve object and authority 
contracts.
• Keyboard, screen-reader, zoom, forced-colors, localization, responsive, low-bandwidth, and reduced-motion variants preserve the same 
strategic meaning.
• One golden journey passes across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise with every permitted variance declared and independently accepted.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE  Design Authority, Scope, and Conformance is conformant only when all UX-01-001 through UX-01-006 close for 
the declared acceptance unit. Volume 8 trace: Ch. 1, Ch. 8, Ch. 71, Ch. 72. Capabilities: CAP-AU-01, CAP-AU-09, CAP-AU-11, CAP-AU-12. Full trace: Appendix 
N.

2  Experience Doctrine and Strategic Intelligence Loop
Experience Doctrine and Strategic Intelligence Loop establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can express Observe, Understand, Predict, Simulate, Decide, and Learn as one continuous operating loop rather than 
disconnected screens or feature islands. It organizes current loop stage, upstream evidence, next governed action, return 
and replay around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human 
authority.
Figure 3. Experience Doctrine and Strategic Intelligence Loop. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Express observe, understand, predict, simulate, decide, and learn as one continuous operating loop rather than disconnected 
screens or feature islands.
Primary roles executives, analysts, operators, domain specialists, administrators, and governance teams.
Controlled objects objective, observation, intelligence object, forecast, scenario, simulation, decision, commitment, outcome, lesson.
Canonical anatomy current loop stage; upstream evidence; next governed action; return and replay.
Primary command Move between loop stages while preserving context, object identity, provenance, work state, and a deterministic return path.
Human authority The experience may recommend a next stage but may not skip a required review, evidence, or human decision gate.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-02-001
The Eye SHALL present Experience Doctrine and Strategic Intelligence Loop as a 
governed experience that enables authorized users to express Observe, Understand, 
Predict, Simulate, Decide, and Learn as one continuous operating loop rather than 
disconnected screens or feature islands; current loop stage, upstream evidence, next 
governed action, return and replay SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental 
presentation.
Design review + journey

content states, visual baselines, and 
accountable design review.

intelligence object, forecast, scenario, simulation, decision, commitment, outcome, 
lesson; active tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario,

remain explicit wherever they change meaning or action.

Positive and negative state fixtures, 
immutable references, trace queries, policy 
decisions, and stale-state evidence.

attributable, recoverable, and replayable.

3  Design Principles and Experience Prohibitions
Design Principles and Experience Prohibitions establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can 
turn the product principles into enforceable interaction rules and prevent chatbot-first, dashboard-first, decorative, certainty-
inflating, or dark-pattern behavior. It organizes principle statement, prohibited behavior, affected pattern, remediation status 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 4. Design Principles and Experience Prohibitions. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, 
human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Turn the product principles into enforceable interaction rules and prevent chatbot-first, dashboard-first, decorative, certainty-inflating, 
or dark-pattern behavior.
Primary roles all product contributors, reviewers, operators, and customer configuration authorities.
Controlled objects principle, prohibition, design decision, exception, review finding, remediation.
Canonical anatomy principle statement; prohibited behavior; affected pattern; remediation status.
Primary command Evaluate a design against explicit principles and record a narrow, expiring exception when conformance cannot yet be achieved.
Human authority No commercial, administrative, model, or delivery role may waive human authority, truth-state separation, provenance, or 
accessibility.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-03-001
The Eye SHALL present Design Principles and Experience Prohibitions as a governed 
experience that enables authorized users to turn the product principles into enforceable 
interaction rules and prevent chatbot-first, dashboard-first, decorative, certainty-inflating, 
or dark-pattern behavior; principle statement, prohibited behavior, affected pattern, 
remediation status SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.
Design review + journey 
test
Approved anatomy, role walkthroughs, 
content states, visual baselines, and 
accountable design review.
UX-03-002
The experience SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile principle, prohibition, 
design decision, exception, review finding, remediation; active tenant, domain, role, 
purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, effective time, object 
identity, freshness, provenance, and policy SHALL remain explicit wherever they change 
meaning or action.
State + trace test
Positive and negative state fixtures, 
immutable references, trace queries, policy 
decisions, and stale-state evidence.
UX-03-003

principles and record a narrow, expiring exception when conformance cannot yet be 
achieved; navigation, search, inspection, drafting, acknowledgment, review, approval, 
publication, and institutional effect SHALL remain visually and behaviorally distinct, 
attributable, recoverable, and replayable.
Interaction + command 
test
Keyboard and pointer journeys, command 
receipts, cancellation, retry, recovery, audit 
causation, and replay evidence.
UX-03-004
The experience SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: No commercial, 
administrative, model, or delivery role may waive human authority, truth-state 
separation, provenance, or accessibility. AI and automation may recommend, forecast, 
simulate, reason, explain, or execute explicitly pre-authorized reversible workflow steps, 
but SHALL NOT infer, preselect, obscure, or synthesize final human decision authority.

consequence test
Eligibility, separation, delegation, recusal,

fixtures.

4  Personas, Roles, Authority, and Consequence Classes
Personas, Roles, Authority, and Consequence Classes establishes the governed experience through which authorized 
users can adapt task density and explanation depth to recurring work while keeping roles, permissions, authority, and 
consequence explicit and separate. It organizes active role, task intent, authority status, consequence class around 
canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 5. Personas, Roles, Authority, and Consequence Classes. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Adapt task density and explanation depth to recurring work while keeping roles, permissions, authority, and consequence explicit and 
separate.
Primary roles the 24 canonical personas, role owners, authority grantors, reviewers, and auditors.
Controlled objects persona, role, permission, authority grant, delegation, recusal, consequence class, task, session.
Canonical anatomy active role; task intent; authority status; consequence class.
Primary command Switch role or delegated mandate explicitly and preview resulting permissions, obligations, and unavailable actions.
Human authority Workspace access, title, administrative privilege, or AI confidence never implies consequential authority.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-04-001
The Eye SHALL present Personas, Roles, Authority, and Consequence Classes as a 
governed experience that enables authorized users to adapt task density and 
explanation depth to recurring work while keeping roles, permissions, authority, and 
consequence explicit and separate; active role, task intent, authority status, 
consequence class SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.
Design review + journey 
test
Approved anatomy, role walkthroughs, 
content states, visual baselines, and 
accountable design review.
UX-04-002
The experience SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile persona, role, 
permission, authority grant, delegation, recusal, consequence class, task, session; 
active tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, 
classification, effective time, object identity, freshness, provenance, and policy SHALL 
remain explicit wherever they change meaning or action.
State + trace test
Positive and negative state fixtures, 
immutable references, trace queries, policy 
decisions, and stale-state evidence.
UX-04-003
The primary command SHALL allow eligible users to switch role or delegated mandate 
explicitly and preview resulting permissions, obligations, and unavailable actions; 
navigation, search, inspection, drafting, acknowledgment, review, approval, publication, 
and institutional effect SHALL remain visually and behaviorally distinct, attributable, 
recoverable, and replayable.
Interaction + command 
test
Keyboard and pointer journeys, command 
receipts, cancellation, retry, recovery, audit 
causation, and replay evidence.
UX-04-004
The experience SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: Workspace access, title, 
administrative privilege, or AI confidence never implies consequential authority. AI and 
automation may recommend, forecast, simulate, reason, explain, or execute explicitly 
pre-authorized reversible workflow steps, but SHALL NOT infer, preselect, obscure, or 
synthesize final human decision authority.
Authority + 
consequence test
Eligibility, separation, delegation, recusal, 
exact digest, consequence preview, 
deliberate action, signature, and denial 
fixtures.

5  Design System Architecture and Governance Model
Design System Architecture and Governance Model establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can govern foundations, components, patterns, templates, content rules, tokens, code implementations, documentation, and 
evidence as one versioned system. It organizes system inventory, dependency graph, release channel, governance queue 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 6. Design System Architecture and Governance Model. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Govern foundations, components, patterns, templates, content rules, tokens, code implementations, documentation, and evidence as 
one versioned system.
Primary roles Design System Authority; component owners; product designers; engineers; accessibility, content, and assurance specialists.
Controlled objects token, primitive, component, pattern, template, package, release, deprecation, contribution, exception.
Canonical anatomy system inventory; dependency graph; release channel; governance queue.
Primary command Propose, review, validate, publish, deprecate, migrate, or retire design assets through recorded ownership and compatibility gates.
Human authority The Design System Authority controls design-system admission; local product teams own conformance within their scope but cannot 
fork canonical semantics.

Canonical Context, Truth, and Product State establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can make 
operating context, object state, truth state, policy state, freshness, authority, and lifecycle visible wherever they can alter 
meaning or action. It organizes context bar, object identity, state strip, policy and freshness around canonical state, 
evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 7. Canonical Context, Truth, and Product State. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, 
human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Make operating context, object state, truth state, policy state, freshness, authority, and lifecycle visible wherever they can alter 
meaning or action.

Controlled objects tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, object, version, truth state, lifecycle state.
Canonical anatomy context bar; object identity; state strip; policy and freshness.
Primary command Inspect or change one context dimension through an explicit preview that identifies affected work, permissions, queries, and pending 
commands.
Human authority Only eligible humans may commit institutional state transitions; AI and automation may propose labeled derived state.

Accessibility, Inclusion, and Global Readiness establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can

Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

Controlled objects preference profile, language, script, modality, focus state, alternative representation, accessibility defect, exception.

Primary command Complete every material journey with keyboard, screen reader, zoom, non-color cues, and role-appropriate language without a 
separate truth.

digest.

8  Cross-Volume Traceability and Design Acceptance
Cross-Volume Traceability and Design Acceptance establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can bind every material visual or interaction decision to constitutional invariants, product requirements, owned capabilities, 
journeys, tests, releases, and accepting authorities. It organizes trace explorer, coverage matrix, evidence status, 
acceptance decision around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit 
human authority.
Figure 9. Cross-Volume Traceability and Design Acceptance. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Bind every material visual or interaction decision to constitutional invariants, product requirements, owned capabilities, journeys, 
tests, releases, and accepting authorities.
Primary roles product, design, engineering, assurance, customer acceptance, audit, and governance authorities.
Controlled objects trace link, requirement, component version, journey, test, finding, evidence, release, acceptance, exception.
Canonical anatomy trace explorer; coverage matrix; evidence status; acceptance decision.
Primary command Resolve any design asset bidirectionally from source authority through implementation and operating evidence to accepted release.
Human authority Independent named authorities accept only the exact scoped evidence package; implementation cannot amend its own authority.

9  Global Application Shell
Global Application Shell establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can provide one persistent, 
role-appropriate operating environment that keeps global context, primary navigation, work state, product health, and 
accountable commands available. It organizes global context, workspace rail, work canvas, status and command layer 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 10. Global Application Shell. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, human authority, and 
continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Provide one persistent, role-appropriate operating environment that keeps global context, primary navigation, work state, product 
health, and accountable commands available.
Primary roles all authenticated product users.
Controlled objects session, tenant, domain, role, workspace, task, object selection, notification, service state.
Canonical anatomy global context; workspace rail; work canvas; status and command layer.
Primary command Enter a workspace, resume governed work, inspect system state, and switch tasks without losing object or authority context.
Human authority The shell may expose consequential commands but never executes them through navigation, dismissal, or context switching.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

layer SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.

10  Operating Context Bar and Scope Switching
Operating Context Bar and Scope Switching establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can show 
and control every context dimension that changes meaning, access, evidence, horizon, scenario, or authority. It organizes 
active scope, changed dimensions, impact preview, commit or cancel around canonical state, evidence, explainability, 
accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 11. Operating Context Bar and Scope Switching. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, 
human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Show and control every context dimension that changes meaning, access, evidence, horizon, scenario, or authority.
Primary roles all users working across customers, domains, objectives, geographies, horizons, scenarios, or classifications.
Controlled objects tenant, domain, role, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, deployment, effective time.
Canonical anatomy active scope; changed dimensions; impact preview; commit or cancel.
Primary command Preview and deliberately commit a context switch while preserving or explicitly discarding affected query, draft, selection, and 
approval state.
Human authority A context switch invalidates any pending approval or command whose digest, eligibility, evidence, or policy scope changes.
Normative design requirements

11  Workspace Navigation and Wayfinding
Workspace Navigation and Wayfinding establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can organize 
durable workspaces around governed outcomes and provide location, hierarchy, recent work, favorites, and cross-
workspace routes without becoming a dashboard catalog. It organizes workspace identity, local navigation, task position, 
cross-workspace route around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit 
human authority.
Figure 12. Workspace Navigation and Wayfinding. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, human 
authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Organize durable workspaces around governed outcomes and provide location, hierarchy, recent work, favorites, and cross-
workspace routes without becoming a dashboard catalog.
Primary roles all personas moving among the 20 canonical workspaces.
Controlled objects workspace, route, section, task, object, saved view, recent item, favorite, breadcrumb, return path.
Canonical anatomy workspace identity; local navigation; task position; cross-workspace route.
Primary command Move within and between workspaces while retaining a visible source, destination, preserved state, and deterministic route back.
Human authority Navigation cannot imply permission, object ownership, review, acknowledgment, approval, publication, or execution.

12  Object-Centered Information Architecture
Object-Centered Information Architecture establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can anchor 
every material surface to canonical objects and versions so presentation cannot invent, duplicate, or obscure strategic state. 
It organizes object header, summary and state, evidence and relationships, actions and history around canonical state, 
evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 13. Object-Centered Information Architecture. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, human 
authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Anchor every material surface to canonical objects and versions so presentation cannot invent, duplicate, or obscure strategic state.
Primary roles object consumers, analysts, stewards, owners, reviewers, and auditors.
Controlled objects canonical object, identifier, version, owner, lifecycle, truth state, relationship, provenance, command.
Canonical anatomy object header; summary and state; evidence and relationships; actions and history.
Primary command Inspect, compare, relate, propose, review, correct, export, or replay an object through commands owned by its canonical service.
Human authority Only the eligible owner or authority may commit a governed object transition; the interface never writes around the owner.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.

Experience outcome Separate discovery, query, navigation, drafting, command, and ai-assisted intent so free-form entry never becomes an ambiguous

Controlled objects query, intent, filter, result set, command, draft, agent request, saved search, history.

18  Typography, Numerical Data, and Content Hierarchy
Typography, Numerical Data, and Content Hierarchy establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can make object identity, evidence, narrative, numerical magnitude, probability, time, units, and commands legible through a 
controlled type and content hierarchy. It organizes page hierarchy, object and metadata, analysis and evidence, numeric 
and machine-readable detail around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and 
explicit human authority.
Figure 19. Typography, Numerical Data, and Content Hierarchy. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Make object identity, evidence, narrative, numerical magnitude, probability, time, units, and commands legible through a controlled 
type and content hierarchy.
Primary roles all users reading or producing strategic content.
Controlled objects type token, text role, label, value, number, unit, timestamp, identifier, digest, locale.
Canonical anatomy page hierarchy; object and metadata; analysis and evidence; numeric and machine-readable detail.
Primary command Scan, read, compare, copy, zoom, or localize content without losing label-value association, precision, or semantic priority.
Human authority Typography may emphasize a recommendation or decision state but cannot visually collapse it into fact or approval.

22  Data Tables, Lists, Trees, and High-Density Inspection
Data Tables, Lists, Trees, and High-Density Inspection establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can support large-scale comparison and inspection while preserving row identity, units, provenance, selection, sort, filter, 
grouping, and action scope. It organizes scope and query, structured header, data body, selection and action summary 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 23. Data Tables, Lists, Trees, and High-Density Inspection. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Support large-scale comparison and inspection while preserving row identity, units, provenance, selection, sort, filter, grouping, and 
action scope.
Primary roles analysts, operators, stewards, administrators, auditors, and executives.
Controlled objects record, row, column, hierarchy node, group, selection, view, filter, sort, aggregation, export.
Canonical anatomy scope and query; structured header; data body; selection and action summary.
Primary command Inspect, sort, filter, group, compare, select, export, or act on exact identified records with visible counts and scope.

26  Evidence, Citation, Provenance, and Chain of Custody
Evidence, Citation, Provenance, and Chain of Custody establishes the governed experience through which authorized users 
can place original evidence, exact cited segments, transformations, rights, custody, quality, and version lineage within one

authority.
Figure 27. Evidence, Citation, Provenance, and Chain of Custody. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, 
command, human authority, and continuity.

evidence experience.

Controlled objects source, evidence item, segment, citation, digest, transformation, custodian, rights, quality assertion, lineage edge.
Canonical anatomy original and segment; citation context; provenance chain; rights and verification.
Primary command Resolve a claim or recommendation to exact evidence bytes and traverse upstream and downstream lineage without losing the point 
of use.

evidence.

41  Executive Operating System Home
Executive Operating System Home establishes the governed experience through which authorized users can organize 
strategic attention around objectives, material changes, warnings, decisions, commitments, outcomes, and learning rather 
than passive metrics. It organizes operating context, priority agenda, decision and commitment flow, outcomes and reset 
around canonical state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 42. Executive Operating System Home. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, human 
authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Organize strategic attention around objectives, material changes, warnings, decisions, commitments, outcomes, and learning rather 
than passive metrics.
Primary roles board members, executives, chiefs of staff, executive operators, and delegated reviewers.
Controlled objects objective, priority, strategic package, warning, decision, commitment, outcome, agenda, operating cadence.
Canonical anatomy operating context; priority agenda; decision and commitment flow; outcomes and reset.
Primary command Review priorities, enter governed work, deliberate decisions, assign commitments, inspect outcomes, and close the strategic loop.
Human authority The executive home summarizes and routes; all consequential decisions occur through exact governed objects and human authority 
gates.

goal into an inspectable bounded plan with tasks, dependencies, tools, data, budgets, checkpoints, gates, stop conditions, 
and expected artifacts. It organizes goal and scope, task graph, resources and gates, review and launch around canonical 
state, evidence, explainability, accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 51. Agent Planner and Task Plan. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, evidence, command, human authority, 
and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Translate a human goal into an inspectable bounded plan with tasks, dependencies, tools, data, budgets, checkpoints, gates, stop 
conditions, and expected artifacts.
Primary roles human requesters, planners, supervisors, domain reviewers, and agent governors.
Controlled objects goal, plan, task, dependency, tool, data reference, budget, checkpoint, gate, stop condition, deliverable.
Canonical anatomy goal and scope; task graph; resources and gates; review and launch.
Primary command Draft, inspect, edit, approve for execution, launch, pause, resume, cancel, or version an agent plan.

human gates.

72  Design Governance, Contribution, Versioning, and Baseline 
Closure
Design Governance, Contribution, Versioning, and Baseline Closure establishes the governed experience through which 
authorized users can operate the design system as a durable product with contribution, review, ownership, release, 
adoption, deprecation, migration, exception, telemetry, learning, and constitutional change control. It organizes contribution 
queue, review and evidence, release and adoption, deprecation and closure around canonical state, evidence, explainability, 
accessibility, recoverable interaction, and explicit human authority.
Figure 73. Design Governance, Contribution, Versioning, and Baseline Closure. The controlled anatomy binds context, primary work, canonical state, 
evidence, command, human authority, and continuity.
Experience contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Experience outcome Operate the design system as a durable product with contribution, review, ownership, release, adoption, deprecation, migration, 
exception, telemetry, learning, and constitutional change control.
Primary roles Design System Authority, Executive Product Authority, contributors, component owners, engineering, assurance, adoption teams, 
and auditors.
Controlled objects contribution, proposal, review, component, package, version, release, adoption, deprecation, migration, exception, change record.
Canonical anatomy contribution queue; review and evidence; release and adoption; deprecation and closure.
Primary command Propose, review, accept, publish, adopt, measure, deprecate, migrate, retire, amend, or close a design-system baseline.
Human authority Design governance may refine expression within the frozen architecture; constitutional meaning changes require explicit amendment 
and ratification.
Normative design requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-72-001
The Eye SHALL present Design Governance, Contribution, Versioning, and Baseline 
Closure as a governed experience that enables authorized users to operate the design 
system as a durable product with contribution, review, ownership, release, adoption, 
deprecation, migration, exception, telemetry, learning, and constitutional change control; 
contribution queue, review and evidence, release and adoption, deprecation and closure 
SHALL precede secondary controls or ornamental presentation.
Design review + journey 
test
Approved anatomy, role walkthroughs, 
content states, visual baselines, and 
accountable design review.
UX-72-002
The experience SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile contribution, proposal, 
review, component, package, version, release, adoption, deprecation, migration, 
exception, change record; active tenant, domain, role, purpose, objective, geography, 
horizon, scenario, classification, effective time, object identity, freshness, provenance, 
and policy SHALL remain explicit wherever they change meaning or action.
State + trace test
Positive and negative state fixtures, 
immutable references, trace queries, policy 
decisions, and stale-state evidence.
UX-72-003
The primary command SHALL allow eligible users to propose, review, accept, publish, 
adopt, measure, deprecate, migrate, retire, amend, or close a design-system baseline; 
navigation, search, inspection, drafting, acknowledgment, review, approval, publication, 
and institutional effect SHALL remain visually and behaviorally distinct, attributable, 
recoverable, and replayable.
Interaction + command 
test
Keyboard and pointer journeys, command 
receipts, cancellation, retry, recovery, audit 
causation, and replay evidence.
UX-72-004
The experience SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: Design governance may 
refine expression within the frozen architecture; constitutional meaning changes require 
explicit amendment and ratification. AI and automation may recommend, forecast, 
simulate, reason, explain, or execute explicitly pre-authorized reversible workflow steps, 
but SHALL NOT infer, preselect, obscure, or synthesize final human decision authority.
Authority + 
consequence test
Eligibility, separation, delegation, recusal, 
exact digest, consequence preview, 
deliberate action, signature, and denial 
fixtures.

ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
UX-72-005
When ownership is missing, contribution evidence is incomplete, adoption diverges, 
deprecation strands consumers, or a proposed change conflicts with authority, the 
experience SHALL reject or hold the change, preserve the prior baseline and consumer 
support, expose impact, and route migration or constitutional adjudication; it SHALL 
NOT represent unavailable, stale, unapproved, unsupported, inaccessible, policy-
filtered, synthetic, or incomplete state as current and complete.

journey

preserved work, disabled unsafe actions,

UX-72-006
Acceptance SHALL demonstrate contribution, ownership, evidence, compatibility, 
adoption, deprecation, migration, exception, rollback, learning, amendment, and closure 
for the exact design-system version, component and pattern versions, capability, 
canonical journey, persona and role, tenant or domain, deployment profile, entitlement, 
theme, locale, device, assistive technology, configuration, data and model versions, 
consequence class, test period, and named accepting authorities.

conformance review

and interaction baselines, accessibility

Failure and degraded experience
PRODUCT CONDITION REQUIRED EXPERIENCE BEHAVIOR
Ownership is missing, contribution evidence is 
incomplete, adoption diverges, deprecation strands 
consumers, or a proposed change conflicts with 
authority.
Reject or hold the change, preserve the prior baseline and consumer support, expose impact, and route 
migration or constitutional adjudication.
Identity, role, authority, policy, classification, purpose, 
or exact command scope cannot be established.
Preserve work and context, deny the consequential effect, identify the unresolved condition, and route eligible 
accountable resolution.
Data, model, agent, integration, projection, network, 
or service state is partial, stale, unavailable, 
inaccessible, or recovering.
Declare visible degraded state, distinguish available from unavailable behavior, retain evidence and checkpoints, 
and recover through verified reconciliation.
Acceptance evidence
• End-to-end evidence demonstrates contribution, ownership, evidence, compatibility, adoption, deprecation, migration, exception, rollback, 
learning, amendment, and closure.
• Positive, negative, stale-state, unauthorized-role, cross-tenant, correction, replay, and recovery fixtures preserve object and authority 
contracts.
• Keyboard, screen-reader, zoom, forced-colors, localization, responsive, low-bandwidth, and reduced-motion variants preserve the same 
strategic meaning.
• One golden journey passes across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise with every permitted variance declared and independently accepted.

through UX-72-006 close for the declared acceptance unit. Volume 8 trace: Ch. 72. Capabilities: CAP-AU-01, CAP-AU-12, CAP-PD-12. Full trace: Appendix N.

values. Customer themes may remap certified aliases but cannot redefine state, authority, or contrast meaning.

B  Semantic State and Truth-State Catalog
Semantic states are expressed through text, symbol, structure, and color. No badge or theme may collapse epistemic, 
authority, lifecycle, severity, or product-health categories.
Truth-state presentation
ID STATE VISIBLE LABEL TOKEN SYMBOL USE CONTRACT
TS-01 Observed OBSERVED color.observed Solid circle Directly recorded evidence; source and time required
TS-02 Asserted ASSERTED color.asserted Outlined square Claim made by an attributable source or actor
TS-03 Inferred INFERRED color.inferred Linked nodes Derived relationship or conclusion; method required
TS-04 Assessed ASSESSED color.assessed Diamond Reviewed analytical judgment with uncertainty
TS-05 Synthetic SYNTHETIC color.synthetic Dashed hexagon Scenario, simulation, or generated state; never observed
TS-06 Recommended RECOMMENDED color.recommended Open arrow Advisory proposal with rationale and alternatives
TS-07 Decided DECIDED color.decided Signed check Human decision bound to authority and digest
TS-08 Corrected CORRECTED color.corrected Revision mark Replacement linked to preserved original
TS-09 Withdrawn WITHDRAWN color.withdrawn Barred circle Not current; identity and history retained
TS-10 Indeterminate INDETERMINATE color.uncertain Question mark Required state cannot be resolved; use constrained
Lifecycle presentation
ID LIFECYCLE STATE BINDING MEANING
LS-01 Draft Authoring; not review-active
LS-02 Proposed Submitted for governed review
LS-03 In Review Named review is active
LS-04 Approved Eligible authority approved exact version
LS-05 Published Approved version made effective for audience
LS-06 Superseded Replaced by a newer effective version
LS-07 Corrected Material correction linked to prior version
LS-08 Withdrawn Removed from current use but preserved
LS-09 Archived Retained for history; no active change
LS-10 Retired No longer supported or admitted
LS-11 Degraded Available with declared limitations
LS-12 Blocked Required condition prevents transition
STATE SEPARATION  Truth state answers what kind of knowledge this is. Lifecycle answers where the governed object is in its controlled evolution. Authority 
answers who may commit a transition. These dimensions remain separate even when shown together.

THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 166
C  Typography, Content, and Numerical Grammar
Typography and content form one semantic hierarchy. The approved UI family is Inter Variable with a system fallback stack; 
machine identifiers use a controlled monospace family. Labels state what an object is, values state its current 
representation, and help text never carries the only material condition.
Content roles
ID ROLE REQUIRED EXPRESSION RULE
CNT-01 Product identity The Eye Never abbreviate in first use or redefine category
CNT-02 Workspace title Noun phrase Names governed work, not a dashboard collection
CNT-03 Object title Canonical human-readable identity Immutable ID remains available
CNT-04 Truth state Approved uppercase label Always paired with symbol and definition
CNT-05 Lifecycle state Approved title-case label Do not combine with truth state
CNT-06 Primary command Verb + object Describe exact effect; avoid generic Continue
CNT-07 Destructive command Specific verb + scope State reversibility and consequence
CNT-08 AI output Proposed / Recommended / Synthetic Name agent or model and non-authority
CNT-09 Uncertainty Plain-language class plus quantitative form State drivers and unsupported precision
CNT-10 Error Condition + affected task + safe recovery Do not blame the user
CNT-11 Warning Severity + condition + scope + owner + action Acknowledgment is not agreement
CNT-12 Degraded mode Available / unavailable / owner / exit Persistent until verified recovery
CNT-13 Permission denial Policy decision and appeal Reveal no protected metadata
CNT-14 Correction Original / replacement / impact / action Reach original recipients
CNT-15 Receipt Actor / command / object / time / result Immutable and copyable
CNT-16 Help Contextual explanation and example Never hides a required condition
Numerical grammar
ID VALUE CLASS DISPLAY BINDING RULE
NUM-01 Probability 0-100% or 0-1 by domain contract Show definition, calibration, horizon, cutoff, and uncertainty
NUM-02 Confidence Named calibrated class or interval Never use as authority or generic certainty
NUM-03 Range Lower to upper with units State method and confidence or scenario basis
NUM-04 Currency Locale display plus ISO code where ambiguity 
exists Preserve canonical value, date, and rate basis
NUM-05 Units Original and canonical units Show conversion method and uncertainty
NUM-06 Date and time User and event zones; canonical instant Show calendar basis and ambiguity
NUM-07 Duration Human-readable plus exact value on inspection Do not mix with timestamp
NUM-08 Large magnitude Locale-aware compact display Exact value remains available; no silent rounding
NUM-09 Precision Horizon- and evidence-appropriate Unsupported decimal precision is prohibited
NUM-10 Missing Not available / not observed / not applicable Never encode missing as zero
NUM-11 Estimate Tilde or explicit Estimated label Show basis and revision state
NUM-12 Negative Minus sign and semantic context Color cannot be the only sign cue

THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM

D  Layout, Grid, Density, and Responsive Contract
The layout system uses a four-pixel base unit, a 12-column standard grid, explicit semantic regions, and five responsive 
display classes. Adaptation may reorder or defer secondary content but never removes context, truth, evidence, uncertainty, 
authority, critical state, or safe recovery.
Responsive display classes
ID CLASS VIEWPORT BINDING COMPOSITION
BP-01 Compact 0-639px Single-column task flow; bottom navigation; no hidden authority or state
BP-02 Narrow 640-959px Single primary canvas with optional modal supporting detail
BP-03 Standard 960-1439px Collapsible rail; 12-column grid; one supporting panel
BP-04 Wide 1440-1919px Persistent rail; primary canvas plus evidence or inspector panel
BP-05 Command 1920px and above Multi-region synchronized views with explicit control surface
Density modes
ID MODE CONTROL AND ROW SCALE PRIMARY USE
DEN-01 Strategic 44px controls; 40-48px rows Executive, decision, briefing, and touch contexts
DEN-02 Operational 36px controls; 32-36px rows Default analytical and administrative work
DEN-03 Inspection 28-32px controls; 28-32px rows High-density tables, traces, and operations centers
Semantic regions
ID REGION PLACEMENT CONTRACT RESPONSIVE RULE
REG-01 Global context Persistent above or before work May collapse to explicit switcher; never disappear
REG-02 Workspace navigation Persistent or recoverable primary wayfinding Preserve location, history, and return path
REG-03 Primary task Largest and first semantic work region One clear task owner per composition
REG-04 Evidence Adjacent, linked, or immediately resolvable Never buried behind unexplained decoration
REG-05 Inspector Selected object detail and state Selection and version remain explicit
REG-06 Action Near affected object or in fixed review region Consequential actions require full digest
REG-07 Status Persistent product and task feedback Distinguish queued, accepted, completed, failed
REG-08 Authority At every consequential gate Name eligibility, review, consequence, and receipt

COMPONENT ADMISSION  A component enters the controlled library only when its semantic purpose is unique, its state machine and action contract are explicit, 
accessibility and localization are certified, visual baselines exist across themes and breakpoints, and an accountable owner accepts lifecycle duty.
=== PAGE 173 ===
THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 173
F  Interaction Pattern Catalog
Patterns define repeatable user intent, state transitions, authority, evidence, recovery, and prohibited shortcuts. Product 
teams compose approved patterns rather than inventing local command semantics.
ID PATTERN BINDING BEHAVIOR PROHIBITION
PAT-01 Inspect without mutation Open exact object/version; preserve context and audit access No side effect on view
PAT-02 Context switch with impact preview Show changed dimensions and affected work before commit Never switch silently
PAT-03 Save draft Persist versioned work without implying submission Never label as approval
PAT-04 Submit for review Freeze review version and name required reviewers Never auto-approve
PAT-05 Acknowledge receipt Record receipt and intended response separately Not agreement or acceptance
PAT-06 Mark reviewed Record reviewer, version, evidence, and findings Not decision authority
PAT-07 Deliberate approval Resolve eligibility, exact digest, rationale, signature, receipt No default or bulk approval
PAT-08 Reject or defer Capture rationale, conditions, owner, and next review Never destructive
PAT-09 Publish exact edition Bind bytes, audience, classification, channel, and approval Export is not publish
PAT-10 Correct and supersede Preserve original and propagate impact Never overwrite history
PAT-11 Withdraw Retain identity, reason, recipients, and replacement Never erase without policy
PAT-12 Compare versions Align changed fields, provenance, authority, and effects No lossy summary only
PAT-13 Compare alternatives Use common objectives, constraints, evidence, and uncertainty No preselected recommendation
PAT-14 Contest a claim Capture counter-evidence, rationale, reviewer, and status Never hide dissent
PAT-15 Request clarification Preserve original intent and ambiguous interpretation No silent model guess
PAT-16 Confirm irreversible action Require typed or re-authenticated deliberate action No generic OK
PAT-17 Preview consequence Show objects, users, reversibility, residual risk, and audit Required before effect
PAT-18 Bulk selection Show exact included, excluded, hidden, and changed counts No select-all ambiguity
PAT-19 Async command receipt Return command ID, accepted scope, status, and safe retry No false completion
PAT-20 Retry idempotently Reuse command identity or require new explicit effect No duplicate side effect
PAT-21 Pause and resume Checkpoint exact state and disclose drift on resume Revalidate permissions
PAT-22 Cancel with residual state Stop new work and show irreversible completed effects Never imply full rollback
PAT-23 Offline queue Show scope, expiry, order, conflicts, and synchronization No silent eventual effect
PAT-24 Resolve conflict Compare base, local, canonical, authority, and consequence No last-write-wins for governed state
PAT-25 Deep link exact state Resolve immutable object/version within current authority No metadata leakage
PAT-26 Return path Restore verified prior context and focus Never repeat a command
PAT-27 Progressive disclosure Defer secondary detail while preserving required state Never hide authority or uncertainty
PAT-28 Empty state Explain scope, evidence status, permissions, and safe next step Never imply zero-world state
PAT-29 Partial result Declare missing sources, intervals, filters, and impact Never present as complete
PAT-30 Degraded mode State available/unavailable behavior, owner, duration, and exit Disable unsafe actions
PAT-31 Permission denial Explain policy and appeal without protected metadata No dead end
PAT-32 Stale state Show source revision, watermark, age, and refresh path No current styling
PAT-33 Agent plan review Inspect goal, tasks, tools, data, budget, gates, and stops Approval remains bounded
PAT-34 Agent tool-call review Show exact tool, parameters, objects, effect, and reversibility No opaque invocation
PAT-35 Agent escalation Show reason, context, alternatives, and requested authority No scope expansion
PAT-36 Agent abstention State unsupported claim, missing evidence, and safe next step Not generic failure
PAT-37 Warning acknowledgment Record receipt separately from action and closure No swipe-away acknowledgment
PAT-38 Warning suppression Require scope, reason, expiry, owner, and visibility No silent mute
PAT-39 Correction notice Link original, replacement, impact, action, and closure Reach original audience
PAT-40 Package installation Show publisher, signature, permissions, evaluation, and diff No one-click hidden permissions
PAT-41 Theme preview Test semantic tokens, contrast, localization, print, and forced colors No raw color override
=== PAGE 174 ===
THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 174
ID PATTERN BINDING BEHAVIOR PROHIBITION
PAT-42 Export preview Show exact scope, format, policy, redaction, and digest Download is attributable
PAT-43 Support access Show purpose, scope, duration, approval, activity, and revoke No standing hidden access
PAT-44 Re-authentication Preserve work and bind assurance to command No unexplained reset
PAT-45 Session handoff Transfer context and control through explicit receipt No authority inheritance
PAT-46 Large-display control Distinguish mirrored view from authenticated control surface Presentation never commands
PAT-47 Learning proposal Separate outcome, attribution, lesson, change, evaluation, approval No silent self-modification
PAT-48 Release acceptance Bind exact scope, versions, evidence, conditions, authorities, and expiry No blanket acceptance
=== PAGE 175 ===
THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 175
G  Strategic Visualization Grammar
Visualizations answer explicit strategic questions and carry their basis, time, units, evidence, uncertainty, scope, and 
accessible alternative. Critical meaning is never hidden behind ornamental visualization.
ID FORM QUESTION REQUIRED DISCLOSURE PROHIBITED USE
VIZ-01 Time series Change through time Time basis, units, source, gaps, uncertainty Line alone without missing 
intervals
VIZ-02 Event timeline Ordered events and decisions Event/record time, actor, source, correction Chronology without time basis
VIZ-03 Probability band Forecast distribution Horizon, cutoff, quantiles, calibration, method Single deterministic line
VIZ-04 Horizon matrix Compare six canonical horizons Target, distribution, method, score, use Mixed horizons on hidden scales
VIZ-05 Scenario fan Plural futures and branches Baseline, branches, divergence, assumptions, signposts One highlighted destiny
VIZ-06 Scenario comparison Compare implications and 
options Common basis, objectives, uncertainty, evidence Unequal comparison frames
VIZ-07 Sensitivity tornado Driver influence Model, baseline, range, units, uncertainty Causal claim without support
VIZ-08 Distribution Range and likelihood Sample/model, units, bins, percentiles, cutoff Decorative smooth curve
VIZ-09 Calibration plot Forecast reliability Period, sample, bins, confidence, method Accuracy score alone
VIZ-10 Risk matrix Exposure by likelihood and 
impact Scales, appetite, uncertainty, owner Color-only heatmap
VIZ-11 Opportunity portfolio Value, timing, confidence, effort Ranges, objective, owner, evidence Bubble area without labels
VIZ-12 Strategic health 
decomposition Score components and weights Formula, weight, evidence, confidence, coverage Gauge without decomposition
VIZ-13 Knowledge graph Entities and typed relationships Identity, edge type/direction/time, evidence, projection Hairball without path purpose
VIZ-14 Strategy Graph Objectives to outcomes Node types, dependencies, status, evidence, owner Generic network diagram
VIZ-15 Dependency network Supply or capability 
dependencies Tier, direction, coverage, criticality, alternatives Unbounded network
VIZ-16 Geospatial map Location and spatial 
relationships CRS, time, scale, source, uncertainty, boundaries Map without accessible table
VIZ-17 Choropleth Normalized regional measure Denominator, breaks, missing data, time, source Absolute count by area
VIZ-18 Flow map Movement or transfer Direction, units, period, source, coverage Width without legend
VIZ-19 Sankey Material flow composition Source/target, conservation rule, units, omissions Decorative flows
VIZ-20 Hierarchy tree Ownership or taxonomy Root, parent relation, version, scope Graph when hierarchy is sufficient
VIZ-21 Treemap Part-to-whole hierarchy Total, unit, nesting rule, accessible table Precise comparison
VIZ-22 Waterfall Contribution to change Start/end, components, sign, units, method Hidden residual
VIZ-23 Variance bridge Actual vs baseline Baseline, actual, drivers, period, source Unattributed delta
VIZ-24 Correlation matrix Association structure Method, sample, time, missingness, caveat Causal language
VIZ-25 Scatter plot Relationship and outliers Axes, units, sample, method, uncertainty Trendline without method
VIZ-26 Control chart Process stability Baseline, limits, rules, sample, intervention Threshold as certainty
VIZ-27 Run comparison Simulation outputs Run manifests, same basis, validity, seed, sensitivity Compare incompatible runs
VIZ-28 Twin state diff Observed or branch changes Parent, time, state class, source/model, validity Mix observed and synthetic
VIZ-29 Decision option matrix Options against objectives Criteria, weights, ranges, dissent, uncertainty Opaque composite rank
VIZ-30 Evidence coverage Support and gaps Source classes, rights, freshness, diversity, omissions Count as quality
VIZ-31 Provenance graph Transformation and custody Artifacts, versions, actors, times, signatures Collapsed lineage
VIZ-32 Agent trace Plan and execution Tasks, models, tools, checkpoints, effects, refusals Animated black box
VIZ-33 Operational status 
board Capability health SLO, freshness, dependencies, degraded contract, owner Green dashboard without state
VIZ-34 Alert queue Priority and ownership Severity, reason, age, owner, acknowledgment, suppression Sort by score only
VIZ-35 Audit timeline Attributable causation Actor, action, object/version, policy, signature, time Editable event stream
VIZ-36 Accessible data 
alternative Non-visual equivalent Summary, table, definitions, units, state, downloadable data Alt text saying chart
VISUALIZATION ACCEPTANCE  Every material chart, map, graph, and simulation view requires a title that states the question, an explicit data and time basis, 
units and definitions, uncertainty and missingness, source and version lineage, legends and non-color cues, keyboard and screen-reader navigation where 
interactive, and an equivalent summary and table.

H  Object, Action, and State Presentation Matrix
The object-action presentation matrix inherits the 40 governed actions from Volume 8. The design system exposes exact 
object identity, eligible actor, preconditions, state effect, evidence, authority, correction, and receipt; presentation cannot 
create an alternate transition.
ID OBJECT ACTION ELIGIBLE ACTOR PRODUCT EFFECT REQUIRED PRESENTATION
OBJ-01 Evidence item View original Any evidence consumer Never mutates source bytes Immutable identity, version, truth, time, 
policy, provenance, no mutation
OBJ-02 Evidence item Annotate Analyst or reviewer Creates a linked versioned 
annotation
Object identity, state, eligible actor, 
intended effect, evidence, receipt, 
recovery
OBJ-03 Evidence item Correct or withdraw Source authority Non-destructive governed case Before/after, authority, reason, impact, 
preserved history, correction path
OBJ-04 Intelligence object Inspect Authorized consumer Shows canonical header and 
status
Immutable identity, version, truth, time, 
policy, provenance, no mutation
OBJ-05 Intelligence object Propose 
assessment Analyst or agent Creates separate derived object
Proposed state, author, evidence, 
uncertainty, review path, no canonical 
effect
OBJ-06 Entity Propose merge Steward or bounded agent No canonical effect before review
Proposed state, author, evidence, 
uncertainty, review path, no canonical 
effect
OBJ-07 Entity Commit merge Eligible steward Versioned reversible canonical 
revision
Exact version, eligibility, consequence 
preview, deliberate action, immutable 
receipt
OBJ-08 Entity Split Eligible steward Preserves prior identity history Before/after, authority, reason, impact, 
preserved history, correction path
OBJ-09 Relationship Propose Analyst or agent Remains a proposal until admitted
Proposed state, author, evidence, 
uncertainty, review path, no canonical 
effect
OBJ-10 Relationship Adjudicate Steward or domain authority Commits, rejects, or qualifies 
proposal
Object identity, state, eligible actor, 
intended effect, evidence, receipt, 
recovery
OBJ-11 Claim Assert Authorized analyst or source 
adapter Explicit asserted truth state
Proposed state, author, evidence, 
uncertainty, review path, no canonical 
effect
OBJ-12 Claim Assess Analyst, method, or agent Creates inference or assessment
Proposed state, author, evidence, 
uncertainty, review path, no canonical 
effect
OBJ-13 Claim Contest Authorized reviewer Preserves competing position
Object identity, state, eligible actor, 
intended effect, evidence, receipt, 
recovery
OBJ-14 Memory item Record Authorized knowledge owner Creates permission-aware version
Object identity, state, eligible actor, 
intended effect, evidence, receipt,

I  Explainability and Human Authority Pattern Library
This library inherits the 20 Volume 8 explanation and authority patterns. Volume 9 controls their mandatory visual order, 
interaction, blocking behavior, and accessible expression; it does not remove or compress the underlying product semantics.
ID PATTERN MANDATORY INFORMATION APPLIES TO BLOCKING 
BEHAVIOR
HX-01 Evidence disclosure Show sources, citations, versions, cutoff, omissions, and policy-
filtered scope
Every assessment, warning, 
forecast, recommendation, and 
briefing
Product may be 
viewed but not marked 
decision-ready
HX-02 Truth-state label Distinguish observed, asserted, inferred, assessed, synthetic, 
recommended, decided, corrected, and withdrawn Every material intelligence object Unsupported 
promotion is blocked
HX-03 Uncertainty statement Show form, magnitude or class, drivers, calibration, and 
unsupported precision
Forecasts, signals, assessments, 
simulations, recommendations
Product is partial or 
abstains
HX-04 Alternative explanations Show plausible alternatives and counter-evidence Material reasoning and causal 
assessments
Independent review 
required
HX-05 Dissent preservation Show reviewer dissent, status, evidence, and disposition Assessments and decision packages Decision-ready status 
requires visible dissent

evaluation AI-assisted strategic products Use constrained when 
method is unresolved
HX-07 Agent trace Show agent identity, plan, context, models, tools, actions, 
checkpoints, and refusals Every governed agent run
Acceptance 
unavailable without 
trace closure

uncertainty, and expected effect Every recommendation
Recommendation 
remains non-decision-
active
HX-09 Human authority banner Name eligible authority, required reviewers, delegation, recusal, 
and current gate
Consequential approval and 
commitment
No eligible authority 
means no transition
HX-10 Exact decision digest Freeze question, options, evidence snapshot, recommendation, 
dissent, conditions, and version Human decision action
Any change 
invalidates pending 
approval
HX-11 AI non-authority notice State that AI recommends, forecasts, simulates, reasons, and 
explains but does not decide AI-assisted decision flow Missing notice blocks

HX-12 Approval action separation Separate review, acknowledgment, approval, rejection, deferral, 
and publication actions All gates and notifications Ambiguous action is 
rejected

Commands, tool calls, decisions, 
commitments

without review

product

means non-
conformance
HX-15 Freshness and coverage 
state

projection watermarks Live and executive views Stale or partial state is 
visibly constrained
HX-16 Degraded-mode contract State available functions, unavailable functions, limitations, 
maximum duration, owner, and exit evidence Every declared degraded mode Unsafe actions 
disabled automatically
HX-17 Policy decision explanation Show identity, purpose, policy version, decision, obligations, denial 
reason, and appeal
Access, privacy, residency, export, 
and model use
Unresolved policy 
means deny or restrict
HX-18 Correction impact notice Link original, correction, affected products, decisions, required 
review, and closure Material correction or withdrawal
Affected products 
cannot claim current 
completeness
HX-19 Override record Capture human authority, reason, evidence, intended effect, 
residual risk, expiry, and review Human override of AI or automation Override without 
authority is denied
HX-20 Outcome and learning 
disclosure
Separate observed outcome, attribution hypothesis, lesson, 
proposed change, and approval Decision Replay and learning No automatic self-
modification
Human decision action contract
CONTROL BINDING EXPERIENCE BEHAVIOR
Eligibility Resolve identity, role, delegation, separation, recusal, purpose, scope, time, session assurance, and required co-approval before enabling 
action.
Exact digest Bind the question, options, evidence cutoff, versions, recommendation, dissent, conditions, intended effect, and current policy decision.
Deliberate action Do not infer approval from navigation, reading, acknowledgment, inactivity, chat text, default selection, bulk action, or administrator 
command.
Rationale and conditions Capture rationale, reservations, conditions, owner, due time, review, and expiry according to consequence class.
Commit and receipt Persist human signature and decision before institutional effect; return immutable receipt, commitments, residual effects, and downstream 
status.

K  Persona and Workspace Experience Matrix
Personas express recurring goals and responsibilities; roles grant permissions; authority permits institutional transitions. 
Workspaces are durable operating environments organized around governed work, not dashboard containers.
Canonical personas
ID PERSONA DECISION AUTHORITY PRIMARY RESPONSIBILITY PROHIBITED 
SUBSTITUTION
