M  Notification, Alert, and Escalation Design Catalog
Notifications transport governed product state; they do not become separate truth sources or approval surfaces. Every 
pattern retains severity, evidence, owner, acknowledgment, escalation, correction, expiry, suppression, and delivery 
receipts.
ID PATTERN PRIORITY CHANNEL RESPONSE AUTHORITY RULE
NOT-01 Information update Low In-app activity stream No Informational only
NOT-02 Assigned task Normal In-app and configured work channel Acknowledge Due time and evidence explicit
NOT-03 Review request Normal In-app and email or enterprise channel Acknowledge Exact object version required
NOT-04 Approval required High In-app plus verified channel Explicit action Never approves from 
notification body
NOT-05 Approval invalidated High In-app plus verified channel Acknowledge Re-review mandatory
NOT-06 Warning issued Consequence-
based Command view, in-app, configured channel Acknowledge Evidence and uncertainty 
included
NOT-07 Warning escalation High or critical Redundant verified channels Acknowledge and act Escalation does not alter 
severity evidence
NOT-08 Warning corrected or withdrawn Same as original Original recipient channels Acknowledge if 
material Links original and replacement
NOT-09 Weak signal nominated Low Analyst queue Disposition Never presented as confirmed 
warning
NOT-10 Source coverage loss High Observation operations and affected owners Acknowledge Affected products and horizons 
shown
NOT-11 Source rights expired Critical Collection, legal, data owners Act Collection and prohibited use 
stop
NOT-12 Evidence quarantined Normal to high Intake review queue Disposition Original custody preserved
NOT-13 Data correction impact High Affected product and decision owners Acknowledge Shows affected versions and 
use
NOT-14 Claim contradiction Normal Analyst and reviewer queue Review Competing claims remain 
visible
NOT-15 Forecast calibration breach High Forecaster, model risk, consumers Acknowledge Decision-active use constrained
NOT-16 Model or agent drift High Governance and capability owners Acknowledge and act Version and affected outputs 
shown
NOT-17 Agent escalation Consequence-
based Requester and eligible supervisor Decide No silent scope expansion
NOT-18 Agent side-effect request High Eligible human gate Explicit action Exact effect digest required
NOT-19 Agent stopped High Requester, supervisor, operations Acknowledge Residual effects and 
checkpoints shown
NOT-20 Simulation run completed Normal Run owner and reviewers Review Validity and limitations included
NOT-21 Simulation validity failed High Run owner, model risk, consumers Acknowledge Output marked non-decision-
active
NOT-22 Twin synchronization lost High Twin owner and affected consumers Acknowledge Observed and synthetic state 
separated
NOT-23 Decision package ready High Eligible decision authority Review Exact digest and expiry shown
NOT-24 Commitment overdue High Owner, sponsor, escalation chain Acknowledge and act Strategic impact linked
NOT-25 Product degraded mode High Affected users and operators Acknowledge when 
consequential
Available and unavailable 
behavior explicit
NOT-26 Recovery state changed High Affected users, operators, customer admins Inform or 
acknowledge
Reconciliation and remaining 
risk shown
NOT-27 Security incident Critical Scoped incident and customer channels Acknowledge and act No speculative scope 
represented as fact
NOT-28 Privacy or residency breach Critical Privacy, customer, legal, incident command Acknowledge and act Jurisdiction and duties explicit
NOT-29 Entitlement or license state Normal to high Customer admins and affected owners Act if service impact Grace and preserved work 
stated
NOT-30 Acceptance condition expiry High Product, customer, assurance authorities Act Capability scope automatically 
constrained
NOT-31 Governance exception expiry High Exception owner and governing authority Act No silent extension
NOT-32 Executive priority change High Affected owners and executive operators Acknowledge Objective, rationale, and 
effective time linked
=== PAGE 185 ===
THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 185
Alert command rules
RULE REQUIRED DESIGN BEHAVIOR
Evidence at action Resolve trigger, evidence, uncertainty, affected objective or exposure, owner, time, correction state, and safe action from the 
canonical object.
Acknowledgment separation Receipt, acknowledgment, review, acceptance, suppression, approval, action, and closure remain separate transitions.
Deterministic escalation Eligibility, delay, retry, redundant channel, substitute role, maximum time, and incident handoff are policy-controlled.
Correction follows recipients Material corrections reach original recipient scope, link prior state, and remain replayable.
Overload is failure Expose precision, duplication, suppression, delivery, ownership, backlog, severe-item visibility, and operator capacity.

Constitutional design coverage
ID INVARIANT NORMATIVE RULE VOLUME 9 CHAPTERS
C-001 Category integrity The Eye shall be conceived, designed, sold, and operated as a Strategic Intelligence Operating System. It shall 
not be reduced to a chatbot, dashboard, BI tool, copilot, or reporting product. Ch. 1-3
C-002 Closed strategic loop The product shall implement one continuous operating loop: Observe, Understand, Predict, Simulate, Decide, 
and Learn. No stage may be treated as a disconnected feature island. Ch. 2, 9, 41
C-003 Product boundary Interfaces such as chat, dashboards, reports, and APIs may exist, but they are access modes to the operating 
system rather than the product definition. Ch. 1-3, 9, 11, 13, 41
C-004 Human decision sovereignty A named human authority shall make every final strategic or high-impact decision. The system may advise, 
prepare, and execute approved workflows, but it shall not silently assume decision rights.
Ch. 3-4, 7, 13, 21, 23, 40, 42, 
46, 48-50, 53-54, 58
C-005 Explain every recommendation Every material forecast, scenario, simulation result, risk, opportunity, and recommendation shall expose 
evidence, assumptions, uncertainty, lineage, alternatives, and accountable system versions.
Ch. 3, 13, 26-27, 33-35, 37-
38, 40, 42-43, 47, 53, 57, 69
C-006 Enterprise operating standard The common platform shall satisfy enterprise requirements for governance, security, scale, auditability, 
reliability, and lifecycle management from its first production architecture. Ch. 5, 66, 71-72
C-007 Universal core, governed 
specialization
A single domain-neutral core shall serve all customer classes. Domain ontologies, models, workflows, agents, 
and policies may extend the core without forking its constitutional semantics. Ch. 5, 10, 20, 36, 66, 68-69
C-008 Ten-layer architecture The ten named architecture layers are the canonical product structure. Cross-cutting planes may support them 
but shall not replace, collapse, or rename them without constitutional amendment. Ch. 5
C-009 Knowledge Graph centrality The Knowledge Graph shall be the connected semantic heart of The Eye and the shared context substrate for 
memory, twins, reasoning, prediction, scenarios, simulations, and decisions. Ch. 12, 25, 29, 36
C-010 Truth-state separation Observed evidence, extracted claims, inferred relationships, model outputs, human judgments, and synthetic 
scenario data shall remain explicitly distinguishable throughout the system.
Ch. 6, 10, 12, 17, 22, 25-27, 
29-31, 36
C-011 Temporal truth Material facts and relationships shall preserve event time, observation time, validity time, and system-record 
time so that past world states can be reconstructed without hindsight contamination. Ch. 6, 15, 25, 28-29, 32, 62
C-012 End-to-end provenance Every material intelligence object shall carry source, chain of custody, transformation history, ownership, rights, 
quality, and version lineage back to its evidence.
Ch. 6, 12, 15, 21-22, 25-26, 
29, 31-32, 42, 46-47, 51, 57, 
62, 64, 66
C-013 Observation breadth The World Observation Layer shall support the full canonical source universe and allow governed custom 
sources without architectural rework. Ch. 31, 36
C-014 Freshness and quality contracts Sources, derived data, twins, models, and intelligence products shall declare measurable freshness, 
completeness, quality, and expiry expectations. Ch. 63

C-015 Durable institutional memory The Eye shall preserve institutional context, decisions, assumptions, evidence, outcomes, and lessons as 
durable memory, subject to lawful retention, deletion, and customer policy. Ch. 30
C-016 Memory governance Memory shall be permission-aware, tenant-isolated, versioned, retrievable, correctable, and protected from 
unverified model output becoming accepted organizational truth. Ch. 30
C-017 Strategy Graph Objectives, hypotheses, decisions, initiatives, capabilities, resources, indicators, risks, opportunities, and 
outcomes shall be connected in a governed Strategy Graph anchored to the Knowledge Graph. Ch. 40, 45
C-018 Grounded Digital Twins Every Digital Twin shall be grounded in evidence, graph entities, temporal state, declared assumptions, and 
calibrated models; it shall never present synthetic state as observed fact. Ch. 39
C-019 Twin versionability Digital Twins shall support time travel, branching, comparison, replay, reconciliation, and versioned evolution 
across actual and simulated states. Ch. 28, 39
C-020 Canonical prediction horizons The Prediction Engine shall support 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons with horizon-
appropriate methods and uncertainty. Ch. 18, 37
C-021 Probabilistic forecasting Forecasts shall communicate distributions, confidence, calibration, drivers, sensitivity, and failure conditions 
rather than false precision or unsupported certainty. Ch. 18, 27, 37, 53
C-022 Scenario plurality Scenario Intelligence shall maintain multiple coherent futures, including baseline, upside, downside, disruption, 
and user-defined alternatives; no single forecast shall masquerade as destiny. Ch. 38
C-023 Reproducible simulation Every simulation shall declare initial state, assumptions, interventions, model versions, constraints, random 
seeds where applicable, outputs, and sensitivity so results can be reproduced and challenged. Ch. 39
C-024 Decision completeness Decision Intelligence shall compare viable options against explicit objectives, constraints, risks, opportunities, 
trade-offs, second-order effects, reversibility, and information value. Ch. 27, 35, 38-40, 45
C-025 Decision Replay The Eye shall preserve the state of knowledge available at decision time and support replay against later 
evidence and outcomes without rewriting the historical record. Ch. 28, 30, 32, 40
C-026 Objective alignment Recommendations shall be evaluated against declared objectives, stakeholder obligations, risk appetite, policy, 
and strategic constraints rather than an opaque global score. Ch. 35, 37, 40, 43, 45, 53
C-027 Executive Operating System The executive surface shall organize strategic attention, decisions, scenarios, evidence, objectives, 
commitments, and outcomes. It shall not degrade into a passive dashboard collection. Ch. 2, 9, 16, 41-42
C-028 Bounded agent authority Every agent shall have a declared purpose, tools, data scope, permissions, budgets, escalation rules, prohibited 
actions, evaluation suite, and accountable owner. Ch. 13, 49-55, 60
C-029 Supervised orchestration Planner, Supervisor, and Workflow agents shall enforce policy, separation of duties, checkpoints, timeouts, 
retries, human gates, and complete execution traces across multi-agent work. Ch. 50-52
C-030 Model pluralism The platform shall support multiple models and reasoning approaches behind governed interfaces so customers 
retain control over cost, sovereignty, performance, and vendor dependence. Ch. 49, 60
C-031 No silent self-modification Continuous learning shall never permit unreviewed production models, agents, prompts, policies, ontologies, or 
decision logic to modify themselves silently. Ch. 50, 52, 56, 60, 72
C-032 Proactive intelligence The system shall surface material changes, weak signals, risks, and opportunities before users ask, while 
respecting relevance, authority, attention, and notification policy. Ch. 33-35, 41, 44, 70
C-033 Early-warning discipline Warnings shall be deduplicated, prioritized, evidence-backed, confidence-aware, time-bounded, and linked to 
affected objectives, assets, decisions, and response playbooks. Ch. 33-34, 44
C-034 Decomposable Strategic Health 
Score
Any Strategic Health Score shall expose component measures, weights, evidence, confidence, trend, and 
sensitivity. The score shall never replace the underlying analysis. Ch. 18, 35, 41, 43
C-035 Trust as a system property Trust, provenance, identity, authorization, policy, safety, and audit shall operate across every layer and 
intelligence object, not as a downstream compliance add-on.
Ch. 26, 49, 55, 57, 59-60, 62, 
67
C-036 Non-repudiable accountability Material access, transformations, agent actions, approvals, decisions, overrides, releases, and exports shall 
produce tamper-evident audit records attributable to identities and versions.
Ch. 4, 15-16, 21-24, 26, 28, 
32, 34, 40, 42, 44-48, 51-52, 
54, 56-58, 61-64, 71
C-037 Customer data control Customers retain governance over their data, models, twins, policies, retention, residency, encryption, exports, 
and deletion within the chosen deployment and contractual boundary. Ch. 58-59, 61, 65-67
C-038 Open interoperability The Eye shall integrate through governed APIs, events, connectors, common data formats, and export paths; 
core customer knowledge shall not be trapped behind proprietary-only access. Ch. 12, 15, 46-47, 64, 67
C-039 Zero-trust security Identity, device, workload, agent, model, source, and data access shall be continuously authenticated, 
authorized, scoped, logged, and re-evaluated under least privilege. Ch. 4, 10, 16, 58-59, 61, 64
C-040 Privacy and minimization Collection and use shall be purpose-bound, proportionate, policy-enforced, and minimized, with support for 
consent, legal basis, access, correction, retention, and deletion obligations. Ch. 58-59, 61, 70
C-041 Sovereignty by design Data residency, key ownership, network isolation, local inference, model choice, administrative control, and 
disconnected operation shall be supported where customer policy requires them. Ch. 48, 58-59, 65
C-042 Deployment semantic parity SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise deployments shall preserve the same core semantics, governance 
model, APIs, artifacts, and intelligence lifecycle, with documented capability differences only where unavoidable. Ch. 48, 64-68, 71
C-043 Resilient operation The platform shall fail safely, degrade intelligibly, recover predictably, preserve evidence and audit continuity, 
and expose when data, models, or dependencies are unavailable or stale.
Ch. 9, 16, 24, 32, 48, 51-52, 
54, 56, 61, 63, 65
C-044 Governed learning loop Learning from outcomes, feedback, overrides, and new evidence shall pass through evaluation, approval, 
release, monitoring, rollback, and tenant-isolation controls. Ch. 56, 60, 72
C-045 Continuous evaluation Models, agents, forecasts, extractors, ontologies, simulations, and recommendations shall be evaluated for 
accuracy, calibration, robustness, drift, bias, safety, cost, and operational fitness.
Ch. 33, 37, 39, 43, 49, 55-56, 
60, 70
C-046 Governed marketplaces Scenario and Agent Marketplace packages shall be signed, versioned, inspectable, permission-scoped, 
licensed, evaluated, sandboxed, monitored, revocable, and traceable to publishers. Ch. 38, 49, 55, 67
C-047 Experience integrity The product experience shall expose context, uncertainty, provenance, decision state, and consequence at the 
point of use; critical meaning shall not be hidden behind ornamental visualization. Ch. 1-3, 5-72

O  UX Design Decision Baseline
These design decisions freeze the UI/UX baseline beneath Volumes 0 and 3-8. Product design, implementation, domain 
specialization, theming, and customer configuration may add precision inside them but cannot use a local choice to change 
strategic meaning or authority.
ADR DECISION BINDING CONSEQUENCE IMPLEMENTATION OBLIGATION

baseline Design refines but does not alter Volumes 0 and 3-8

components and patterns, canonical objects, explicit 
state, accessible journeys, fault behavior, trace 
evidence, and scoped acceptance.

operating system

BI, copilot, or report

evidence, and scoped acceptance.

P  Design System Glossary and Release Checklist
The glossary fixes Volume 9 terminology. The release checklist is a mandatory summary gate; it supplements rather than 
replaces requirement-level evidence and named acceptance.
Design system glossary
TERM CONTROLLED DEFINITION
Accessible alternative A non-visual or alternate-modality representation that preserves task, meaning, state, and authority.
Action scope The exact objects, versions, users, and effects to which a command applies.
Application shell The persistent context, navigation, work, status, and command environment around all workspaces.
Authority banner A component naming eligible authority, reviewers, delegation, recusal, and current gate.
Canonical anatomy The required ordered regions and information roles of a component, pattern, or surface.
Command An explicit request to an owning service that may change governed state and produces an attributable receipt.
Command surface The authenticated interface that can issue commands; mirrored views are non-authoritative.
Component A reusable accessible implementation of a bounded visual and interaction contract.
Consequence class A policy classification controlling explanation, review, authority, friction, evidence, and recovery requirements.
Consequence preview A pre-action view of intended effects, affected objects, reversibility, residual risk, and audit scope.
Context bar The persistent control showing tenant, domain, role, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, and time.
Decision digest The immutable question, options, evidence cutoff, recommendation, dissent, conditions, intended effect, and versions bound 
to a human action.
Degraded mode A declared state with explicit available behavior, limitations, maximum duration, owner, and exit evidence.
Density mode A controlled spacing and sizing profile that changes information density without changing meaning or capability.
Design asset A token, primitive, component, pattern, template, content rule, package, implementation, or evidence artifact.
Design authority The named body accountable for design-system admission, versioning, conformance, exception, and retirement.
Design token A named semantic value that controls a reusable visual or interaction property.
Dissent An attributable competing assessment or objection retained with evidence, state, reviewer, and disposition.
Evidence region The surface area that resolves claims, recommendations, or state to exact sources, versions, and provenance.
Experience contract The outcome, context, object, interaction, authority, failure, continuity, and acceptance behavior a surface must preserve.
Focus origin The control or object from which a layered interaction began and to which focus returns.
Freshness watermark A persistent label stating data cutoff, age, source health, projection revision, and permitted use.
Global context The operating scope that applies across workspaces and can alter meaning, permission, or authority.
Golden journey A canonical end-to-end journey used as a parity and acceptance fixture across roles, modes, and deployments.
Human gate A transition that requires an eligible human to review and deliberately act on an exact digest.
Indeterminate A state in which required identity, evidence, policy, time, trust, or authority cannot be resolved.
Interaction pattern A reusable sequence and state contract for completing a class of user intent.
Material journey A journey whose failure changes strategic meaning, access, evidence, authority, safety, or institutional effect.
Non-color cue Text, symbol, position, pattern, or structure that communicates meaning independently of color.
Object header The canonical identity, version, state, time, owner, policy, trust, and action region for an object.
Optimistic UI Presentation of an unconfirmed state as complete before authoritative receipt; prohibited for consequential state.
Pattern A governed composition of components, states, actions, content, and accessibility behavior.
Permitted use The decision, workflow, or analytical uses allowed by current evidence, truth, policy, freshness, and authority state.
Priority queue An explainable owned ordering of material items with reason, severity, due time, capacity, and suppression history.
Progressive disclosure Deferral of secondary detail without hiding required context, state, evidence, uncertainty, or authority.
Projection watermark A label identifying the canonical source revision and lag of a derived view.
Receipt An immutable attributable record of command acceptance, effect, denial, delivery, or terminal state.
Responsive composition An approved layout transformation that preserves semantic regions, focus order, and material information.
=== PAGE 195 ===
THE EYE                                                       VOLUME 9 / UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED UI/UX DESIGN BASELINE V1.0                               PAGE 195
TERM CONTROLLED DEFINITION
Semantic parity Identical outcome, object, authority, explanation, correction, replay, and failure meaning across implementations.
Semantic token A token named by purpose or state rather than raw visual value.
Strategic visualization grammar Rules binding a visual form to question, data, basis, uncertainty, provenance, and accessible alternative.
Surface A bounded visual region that groups related context, work, evidence, or actions.
Synthetic state Scenario, simulation, generated, or modeled state that remains explicitly separate from observed state.
Theme A certified mapping from semantic tokens to approved visual values for a mode or customer brand.
Truth state The explicit epistemic category of an object, claim, relationship, or output.
Visual baseline An approved rendered reference used for automated and human regression review.
Workspace A durable operating environment organized around governed work, objects, roles, and outcomes.
Release checklist
ID GATE REQUIRED CLOSURE
REL-01 Authority Every material design asset has one accountable owner and approved status.
REL-02 Traceability Requirements, components, patterns, journeys, tests, findings, and releases resolve bidirectionally.
REL-03 Context Tenant, domain, role, objective, horizon, scenario, classification, and time remain explicit where material.
REL-04 Object identity Canonical object, version, owner, lifecycle, truth, policy, and provenance are resolvable.
REL-05 Human authority Eligibility, separation, digest, consequence, deliberate action, signature, and receipt pass.
REL-06 AI non-authority Agent and model outputs remain labeled, explainable, contestable, and non-decision-active.
REL-07 Truth states Observed, asserted, inferred, assessed, synthetic, recommended, decided, corrected, and withdrawn are distinct.
REL-08 Evidence Material claims and actions resolve to exact evidence, versions, rights, quality, and lineage.
REL-09 Uncertainty Magnitude, drivers, calibration, alternatives, dissent, and unsupported precision are visible.
REL-10 Actions Navigation, draft, acknowledgment, review, approval, publication, and effect are distinct.
REL-11 Recovery Cancel, retry, correction, rollback, replay, and partial-effect paths are tested.
REL-12 Degraded modes Every dependency failure exposes limitations, safe behavior, owner, recovery, and exit evidence.
REL-13 Accessibility Material journeys pass automated, manual, assistive-technology, keyboard, zoom, and cognitive review.
REL-14 Localization Text expansion, RTL, complex scripts, locale formats, terminology, and translation provenance pass.
REL-15 Responsive Compact, narrow, standard, wide, command, zoom, orientation, and large-display compositions pass.
REL-16 Themes Light, dark, forced colors, print, customer theme, and fallback theme pass semantic certification.
REL-17 Visualizations Every material visual has basis, units, time, source, uncertainty, legend, and accessible alternative.
REL-18 Performance Loading, progress, cancellation, timeout, duplicate, perceived latency, and low-bandwidth behavior pass.
REL-19 Security and privacy Identity, purpose, least privilege, classification, redaction, support access, and audit pass.
REL-20 Deployment parity SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise golden journeys preserve semantic parity.
REL-21 Offline Freshness, expiry, queued effects, revocation, conflict, synchronization, secure wipe, and audit pass.
REL-22 Content Canonical terminology, labels, instructions, errors, warnings, and corrections pass comprehension review.
REL-23 Telemetry Instrumentation has purpose, consent, lineage, coverage, privacy review, and metric governance.
REL-24 Acceptance Exact scope, versions, conditions, evidence age, findings, authorities, signatures, and expiry are retained.
BASELINE CLOSURE  Volume 9 closes only when all 432 UX requirements, 52 constitutional invariants, 72 chapter acceptance units, inherited Volume 8 
contracts, design assets, accessibility and parity evidence, findings, exceptions, and named authorities resolve to this controlled edition without concealed conflict.
